18	
19	# 论文写作风格规范
20	
21	> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。
22	
23	---
24	
25	## 一、总体写作哲学
26	
27	### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌
28	
29	- 论文必须以"故事线"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证
30	- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进
31	- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌
32	- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing
33	
34	### 1.2 主线统一性
35	
36	- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**
37	- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C
38	- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机
39	- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线
40	
41	### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义
42	
43	- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释
44	- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）
45	- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用
46	- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug
47	
48	### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文
49	
50	- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可
51	- 重点学：
52	  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事
53	  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献
54	  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）
55	  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度
56	- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大
57	- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文
58	
59	---
60	
61	## 二、Abstract 写作规范
62	
63	### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）
64	
65	Abstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：
66	1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点
67	2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）
68	3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）
69	4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献
70	
71	### 2.2 禁忌
72	
73	- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim
74	- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 "revolutionary"、"groundbreaking"
75	- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元
76	- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了
77	- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开
78	
79	### 2.3 审稿人视角检验
80	
81	写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——
82	- 问题是否重要？
83	- 方法是否清楚？
84	- 贡献是否可信？
85	- 结果是否有说服力？
86	
87	---
88	
89	## 三、Introduction 写作规范
90	
91	### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）
92	
93	Introduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：
94	
95	1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈
96	2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题
97	3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）
98	4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题
99	5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献
100	
101	### 3.2 整体叙事结构
102	
103	```
104	现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结
105	```
106	
107	### 3.3 禁忌
108	
109	- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 "LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities..."，要从具体问题切入
110	- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接
111	- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 "我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果" 的罗列
112	- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
113	- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念
114	
115	---
116	
117	## 四、Related Work 写作规范
118	
119	### 4.1 目标
120	
121	让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。
122	
123	### 4.2 结构要求
124	
125	- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）
126	- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系
127	- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口
128	
129	### 4.3 禁忌
130	
131	- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 "A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z." 的列表
132	- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较
133	- Contribution claim（"To our knowledge, no prior work..."）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work
134	
135	### 4.4 引用充足度
136	
137	- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**
138	- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分
139	- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足
140	
141	---
142	
143	## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）
144	
145	以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**
146	
147	### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）
148	
149	**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**
150	
151	- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**
152	- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子
153	- 强制检查命令：
154	  ```bash
155	  grep -n "—" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要
156	  grep -n " – " paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除
157	  ```
158	- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）
159	
160	### 5.2 段落与句式
161	
162	- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的
163	- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句
164	- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 "..., highlighting the applicability of..." / "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一
165	- **禁止连续使用 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词
166	- **避免 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."**：直接写要说的内容
167	
168	### 5.3 用词黑名单
169	
170	- 避免 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "facilitate" — 用 "explore" / "use" / "enable"
171	- 避免 "a myriad of" / "a plethora of" — 用 "many" / "various"
172	- 避免 "in the realm of" / "in the context of" — 直接说
173	- 避免 "paradigm shift" / "novel" 过度使用
174	
175	### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）
176	
177	1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写
178	2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确
179	3. **`grep "—"` + `grep " – "` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）
180	4. `grep "ing,"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写
181	
182	---
183	
184	## 六、图表与视觉规范
185	
186	### 6.1 知识密度原则
187	
188	- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间
189	- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中
190	- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure
191	
192	### 6.2 表格规范
193	
194	- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）
195	- 小数精度全表统一
196	- 最优加粗、次优下划线
197	- Caption 必须自包含
198	
199	### 6.3 图片规范
200	
201	- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签
202	- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）
203	- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求
204	- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页
205	
206	### 6.4 空间效率
207	
208	- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源
209	- 优先使用 `\subfigure` 合并相关图
210	- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动
211	- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比
212	
213	### 6.5 页面节奏
214	
215	好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：
216	
217	- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉"三步展开
218	- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏
219	- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float
220	- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）
221	- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）
222	
223	### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范
224	
225	**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：
226	- 用 `\toprule` / `\midrule` / `\bottomrule`，禁止 `\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`
227	- 用 `\centering` 不用 `\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）
228	- 最优结果加粗（`\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向
229	- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）
230	- Caption 在表格上方（`\caption` 在 `\begin{tabular}` 之前）
231	
232	**宽度决策树**：
233	- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\columnwidth`）
234	- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）
235	- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix
236	
237	---
238	
239	## 七、投稿前通用准备
240	
241	### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）
242	
243	- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）
244	- **不要用**：
245	  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）
246	  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）
247	  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）
248	- 风险：
249	  - 样式不符合 → desk reject
250	  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信
251	  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范
252	- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch "{venue} {year} latex template official"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）
253	- `\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致
254	
255	### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对
256	
257	- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A
258	- 页数限制：查目标会议官网
259	- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同
260	- 行号开启（review 版常需）
261	- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件
262	
263	### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程
264	
265	提交前必须逐一检查：
266	1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入
267	2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态
268	3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事
269	4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）
270	5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）
271	6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）
272	7. 所有专有名词有定义
273	8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）
274	9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡
275	10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础
276	
````

      "content": "---\nname: paper-writing-norms\ndescription: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。\nallowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]\nscope: global\nversion: 2\npriority: 9\ntags:\n  - writing\n  - paper\n  - style\n  - norms\nroles:\n  - agent\n  - worker\n  - paper_agent\n---\n\n# 论文写作风格规范\n\n> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。\n\n---\n\n## 一、总体写作哲学\n\n### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌\n\n- 论文必须以\"故事线\"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证\n- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进\n- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌\n- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing\n\n### 1.2 主线统一性\n\n- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**\n- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C\n- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机\n- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线\n\n### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义\n\n- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释\n- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）\n- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用\n- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug\n\n### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文\n\n- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可\n- 重点学：\n  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事\n  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献\n  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）\n  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度\n- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大\n- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文\n\n---\n\n## 二、Abstract 写作规范\n\n### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）\n\nAbstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：\n1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点\n2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）\n3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）\n4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献\n\n### 2.2 禁忌\n\n- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim\n- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 \"revolutionary\"、\"groundbreaking\"\n- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元\n- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了\n- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开\n\n### 2.3 审稿人视角检验\n\n写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——\n- 问题是否重要？\n- 方法是否清楚？\n- 贡献是否可信？\n- 结果是否有说服力？\n\n---\n\n## 三、Introduction 写作规范\n\n### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）\n\nIntroduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：\n\n1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈\n2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题\n3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）\n4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题\n5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献\n\n### 3.2 整体叙事结构\n\n```\n现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结\n```\n\n### 3.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 \"LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities...\"，要从具体问题切入\n- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接\n- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 \"我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果\" 的罗列\n- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念\n\n---\n\n## 四、Related Work 写作规范\n\n### 4.1 目标\n\n让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。\n\n### 4.2 结构要求\n\n- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）\n- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系\n- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口\n\n### 4.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 \"A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z.\" 的列表\n- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较\n- Contribution claim（\"To our knowledge, no prior work...\"）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work\n\n### 4.4 引用充足度\n\n- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**\n- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分\n- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足\n\n---\n\n## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）\n\n以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**\n\n### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n\n**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**\n\n- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**\n- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子\n- 强制检查命令：\n  ```bash\n  grep -n \"—\" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要\n  grep -n \" – \" paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除\n  ```\n- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）\n\n### 5.2 段落与句式\n\n- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的\n- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句\n- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 \"..., highlighting the applicability of...\" / \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一\n- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词\n- **避免 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"**：直接写要说的内容\n\n### 5.3 用词黑名单\n\n- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"facilitate\" — 用 \"explore\" / \"use\" / \"enable\"\n- 避免 \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\" — 用 \"many\" / \"various\"\n- 避免 \"in the realm of\" / \"in the context of\" — 直接说\n- 避免 \"paradigm shift\" / \"novel\" 过度使用\n\n### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）\n\n1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写\n2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确\n3. **`grep \"—\"` + `grep \" – \"` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）\n4. `grep \"ing,\"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写\n\n---\n\n## 六、图表与视觉规范\n\n### 6.1 知识密度原则\n\n- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间\n- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中\n- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure\n\n### 6.2 表格规范\n\n- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）\n- 小数精度全表统一\n- 最优加粗、次优下划线\n- Caption 必须自包含\n\n### 6.3 图片规范\n\n- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签\n- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）\n- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求\n- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页\n\n### 6.4 空间效率\n\n- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源\n- 优先使用 `\\subfigure` 合并相关图\n- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动\n- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比\n\n### 6.5 页面节奏\n\n好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：\n\n- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用\"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉\"三步展开\n- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏\n- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float\n- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）\n- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）\n\n### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范\n\n**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：\n- 用 `\\toprule` / `\\midrule` / `\\bottomrule`，禁止 `\\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`\n- 用 `\\centering` 不用 `\\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）\n- 最优结果加粗（`\\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向\n- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）\n- Caption 在表格上方（`\\caption` 在 `\\begin{tabular}` 之前）\n\n**宽度决策树**：\n- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\\columnwidth`）\n- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）\n- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix\n\n---\n\n## 七、投稿前通用准备\n\n### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）\n\n- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）\n- **不要用**：\n  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）\n  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）\n  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）\n- 风险：\n  - 样式不符合 → desk reject\n  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信\n  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范\n- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch \"{venue} {year} latex template official\"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）\n- `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致\n\n### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对\n\n- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A\n- 页数限制：查目标会议官网\n- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同\n- 行号开启（review 版常需）\n- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件\n\n### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程\n\n提交前必须逐一检查：\n1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入\n2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态\n3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事\n4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）\n5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）\n6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）\n7. 所有专有名词有定义\n8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）\n9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡\n10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础\n",
      "numLines": 276,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 276
    }
  },

20	# 论文写作质量指南（NeurIPS 实战经验）
21	
22	> 来源：2026 年 NeurIPS 投稿期间，从 20+ 项目的人工审查反馈中提炼。
23	> 每条规则都源自真实的 bug / 退回 / 审稿质疑。
24	
25	---
26	
27	## 一、数据诚信与溯源（最高优先级）
28	
29	### 1.1 数字必须有源头
30	
31	- 论文中**每一个数字**必须可追溯到 registry 实验 ID 或原始结果文件
32	- 禁止凭记忆写数字——必须 `cat result.json | head` + `ls -la` 附上文件证据
33	- 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）必须独立重算，不信 Agent 第一手汇报
34	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 覆盖率必须 100%，改数字必须同步更新 provenance
35	
36	> **教训（D149 事件）**：Agent 曾伪造完整的 metrics 数据包括 p-value，事后被 Reviewer 独立 parse JSON 抓出。从此所有关键数据必须 Reviewer 独立验证到字段级别。
37	
38	### 1.2 不同来源数据不能混用
39	
40	- 配对数据必须同 seed、同 c 值、同 N、同 evaluation protocol
41	- 两张表如果 protocol 不同（如 0-shot vs 8-shot），论文必须明确说明
42	- 跨 config 比较 delta 会引入系统误差——必须补跑同 config baseline 后才能比较
43	- 非等量对比必须显式标注（如 "at unmatched volumes"）
44	
45	> **教训（D027 事件）**：不同 config 文件的 baseline 不同，跨 config 比较 delta 值引入了 artifact。
46	
47	### 1.3 结论撤回后不可复活
48	
49	- 被 Director shelved 的数据（如因截断混淆），禁止重新引入论文
50	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 中 `verified: false` 的 claim，不得恢复
51	- 术语修正后不可回退（如 "same norm" → "same per-layer standard deviation"）
52	
53	---
54	
55	## 二、Claim 强度与数据匹配
56	
57	### 2.1 Claim 强度规则
58	
59	| 数据支撑 | 允许的最强表述 |
60	|----------|--------------|
61	| 单 seed, 单数据集 | "preliminary observation", "initial evidence" |
62	| 单 seed, 多数据集 | "suggestive pattern", "indicative trend" |
63	| 多 seed, 单数据集 | 报告均值±标准差，可写 "we find" |
64	| 多 seed, 多数据集 | 可用 "robust", "consistent" |
65	| 统计检验显著 | 可用 "significant", 附 p-value |
66	| 单 seed + 外推到其他条件 | 必须加限定，不能声称 "scale-invariant" 等 |
67	
68	### 2.2 禁止的过强表述
69	
70	- "remarkably stable" → 改为 "relatively stable" 或给出量化波动范围
71	- "confirmed" → 仅 "suggestive" 级别数据不能用
72	- "scale-invariant" → single-seed 验证不够
73	- "We attribute X to Y" → 如果是假说，必须改为 "We hypothesize" + "requiring causal verification"
74	- "roughly twice" → 实际比值必须在 1.8x-2.2x 才能用，否则改为更准确的近似
75	- "domain-agnostic" → 改为 "robust across N domains tested"
76	
77	### 2.3 Claim 降级机制
78	
79	当证据不足以支撑独立 claim 时，应降级为 discussion 中的定性讨论，而非删除。具体做法：
80	- 独立 contribution → Discussion 段落
81	- 硬 claim → 加 caveat 的软 claim
82	- 正文数字 → 移入 Appendix
83	
84	---
85	
86	## 三、叙事与 Storytelling
87	
88	### 3.1 结构原则
89	
90	- 核心 paradox/发现之间必须有**过渡句**桥接，不能让读者在关键处断线
91	- Introduction 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
92	- Discussion 不应重复 Results 的具体数字——引用表格编号
93	- 同一解释不要在多处重复推导——主文写一次 + 指向 Appendix
94	- Limitations 段不要写成一个 17 行大段——拆分为 2-3 个子段按主题分组
95	
96	### 3.2 冷引用（Cold Reference）
97	
98	- 任何首次出现的概念必须有上文铺垫
99	- Introduction 贡献列表中不能突然出现未被提及的模型/方法名
100	
101	### 3.3 不要用旧叙事套新数字
102	
103	新 baseline 出来后若 delta 大幅变化，Section 的叙事逻辑必须重写，不是简单替换数字。
104	
105	### 3.4 Contingency Plan
106	
107	关键实验结果不确定时需预写两套叙事（如 "若结果 A → 叙事 A"、"若结果 B → 叙事 B"），实验完成后选择正确版本。提交前必须确认只留了一套。
108	
109	### 3.5 不要基于中间值做叙事决策
110	
111	70% 进度时的估计往往不够稳定（尤其 GSM8K 等难度分布不均匀的 benchmark），必须等完整数据再定稿叙事。
112	
113	---
114	
115	## 四、术语与一致性
116	
117	### 4.1 术语一致性
118	
119	- 全文必须统一使用正式定义的术语，不得出现同义词漂移
120	- 术语定义后，每次使用必须完全一致（不要有时 "perturbation sensitivity" 有时 "perturbation impact"）
121	
122	### 4.2 统计量一致性
123	
124	- 两个不同的 kappa/p-value 每次出现时必须标注是哪个
125	- 同一个 metric 在不同位置的数字必须完全一致
126	- 修改任何数字后必须全文 grep 确认所有引用处同步
127	
128	### 4.3 p-value 格式
129	
130	- 无前导零、3 位小数：`p = .003`（不是 `p = 0.003`）
131	- 当非劣效性 p<0.001 实际由正向效应驱动时，必须加括注说明
132	
133	### 4.4 例子与定义必须一致
134	
135	Taxonomy 中的定义和对应示例不能自相矛盾。如果定义说 "no new content"，示例中就不能包含新信息。
136	
137	---
138	
139	## 五、投稿前检查清单（Pre-Submission Checklist）
140	
141	### 5.1 占位符扫描（硬门禁）
142	
143	提交前必须 grep 扫描，以下模式要求**零匹配**：
144	```
145	XX.XX, PLACEHOLDER, [If , TODO, TBD, \placeholder{
146	```
147	所有条件占位段落（`[If ...]`）必须替换为最终文本。
148	
149	### 5.2 编译检查
150	
151	- [ ] 编译零 error
152	- [ ] 无 `??` 未解析引用
153	- [ ] 无 > 1pt 的 overfull hbox
154	- [ ] 正文 ≤ 9 页（NeurIPS desk reject 风险）
155	- [ ] 使用当年 style file（`neurips_2026.sty`）
156	- [ ] Review 版启用行号
157	- [ ] 匿名审稿版无作者姓名、无致谢
158	
159	### 5.3 数据完整性
160	
161	- [ ] 论文所有数字与 registry/provenance 交叉验证通过
162	- [ ] 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）独立重算确认
163	- [ ] 所有 evaluation protocol 差异已在论文中说明
164	- [ ] 多 seed 结果报告均值和标准差
165	- [ ] 单 seed 数据在 Limitations 中声明
166	
167	### 5.4 图表检查
168	
169	- [ ] 所有 figure PDF 存在且路径匹配
170	- [ ] 图表不超栏宽（单栏 5.5in / 全宽 6.5in）
171	- [ ] 图中字体 ≥ 7pt
172	- [ ] 小数精度全表统一
173	- [ ] 最优结果加粗、次优下划线
174	- [ ] 表格标题（caption）自包含
175	- [ ] Forest plot 等可视化无 dummy 值（CI_LO=-99 等）
176	
177	### 5.5 引用检查
178	
179	- [ ] 引用覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**（任一缺失说明调研不充分）
180	- [ ] 引用密度与目标会议近年录用论文同量级
181	- [ ] 引用键与 bib 条目 1:1 匹配
182	- [ ] 无孤儿 bib 条目
183	- [ ] 无幻觉引用（所有被引论文实际存在）
184	- [ ] 近年论文的年份/会议准确
185	- [ ] 审稿人要求的引用全部补齐
186	
187	### 5.6 匿名化检查
188	
189	- [ ] 源代码无硬编码绝对路径（`/Users/...`, `/root/...`）
190	- [ ] 配置文件无服务器路径
191	- [ ] Git history 已清理 author 信息（用 fresh repo）
192	- [ ] 项目管理文件不得提交（AGENTS.md, memory.md, registry.yaml, task_context/ 等）
193	- [ ] API Keys 通过环境变量，无泄露
194	
195	### 5.7 会议 Checklist 填写（如目标会议要求）
196	
197	- [ ] Claims: 摘要/Introduction 准确反映实验范围
198	- [ ] Limitations: 正文 Limitations 段 + Appendix 补充
199	- [ ] Experiments: 实验设置完整（exp_id, seed, 超参, 评估 protocol）
200	- [ ] Reproducibility: 数据集公开、代码可用
201	- [ ] Statistical Significance: 多 seed 均值+标准差、置信区间
202	- [ ] Compute: GPU 型号、训练时间、总 GPU-hours
203	- [ ] Licenses: 数据集/模型引用完整
204	- [ ] AI Assistants: 如实声明使用情况
205	- [ ] 每项认真回答（不要全写 N/A）
206	
207	### 5.8 叙事完整性
208	
209	- [ ] Conclusion 包含所有关键限定符（如 "under greedy decoding"）
210	- [ ] Abstract 与正文一致（不能一个写 "single seed" 另一个已是 "10 seeds"）
211	- [ ] 无 Appendix Version A/B 两套叙事残留
212	- [ ] 无 LaTeX 注释与正文混淆
213	- [ ] Contribution claim 在 Introduction 中（不在 Related Work 中）
214	- [ ] 所有专有名词/缩写首次出现时有定义（Abstract 内缩写也必须展开）
215	- [ ] Introduction 与 Method/Results 讲同一条主线（idea 变化后全文逻辑一致）
216	
217	### 5.9 去 AI 风格检查
218	
219	- [ ] **`grep "—" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 em-dash 必须全删，最显著的 AI 痕迹）
220	- [ ] **`grep " – " paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 en-dash 必须全删）
221	- [ ] 无 "-ing," 分词短语结尾（如 "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."）
222	- [ ] 无连续 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"（同段最多 1 个）
223	- [ ] 无 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."
224	- [ ] 无 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "a myriad of" / "a plethora of"
225	- [ ] 自然段 5-8 句是正常的，不要每 2-3 句就换段
226	
227	### 5.10 模板与同类论文核对
228	
229	- [ ] LaTeX 模板从目标会议官网下载（不是他人转载、不是上一年）
230	- [ ] `\documentclass[]{}` 选项匹配 review / camera-ready 阶段
231	- [ ] 写作前已参考 2-3 篇同领域近 1-2 年优秀论文，叙事节奏对齐
232	
233	---
234	
235	## 六、Reviewer 审查标准
236	
237	### 6.1 CRITICAL 级别（必须修复才能提交）
238	
239	- 论文数字与实验数据不一致
240	- Abstract 与正文矛盾
241	- Evaluation protocol 混淆（如 0-shot 写成 8-shot）
242	- 未经验证的数据写入论文
243	- 占位符残留
244	
245	### 6.2 WARNING 级别（应当修复）
246	
247	- Stale claims（更新了某处数字但忘了同步其他位置）
248	- 过强措辞不匹配数据支撑
249	- 隐式全因子设计误导（如写 "3×3×3=27" 但实际只有 15 格）
250	- 评估指标只描述了部分 domain
251	- 旧版数据引用
252	
253	### 6.3 数据一致性审查流程
254	
255	1. 从 registry 提取所有实验 metrics
256	2. 对照论文中每一个内联数字（到行号级别）
257	3. 独立重算所有衍生值
258	4. 不一致处标注为 CRITICAL
259	5. 近似表述检查实际比值是否在允许范围内
260	
261	---
262	
263	## 七、常见反模式速查表
264	
265	| 反模式 | 症状 | 修复方式 |
266	|--------|------|----------|
267	| Stale claim | 论文某处还引用旧数字 | 改数字后全文 grep 同步 |
268	| Protocol 混淆 | 不同表格用了不同 evaluation 但没说明 | 每张表注明 protocol |
269	| Agent 误报 | 实验结果缺乏文件证据 | 要求 `cat result.json` 截图 |
270	| 混用数据算比值 | 跨 seed/条件/N 的数据混合比较 | 只用严格配对的数据 |
271	| 叙事超越数据 | "confirmed" 但实际只是 suggestive | 匹配 claim 强度到 §2.1 |
272	| 冷引用 | 首次出现的概念无铺垫 | 先建立框架再引入 |
273	| 定义-例子矛盾 | Taxonomy 定义和示例冲突 | 审查所有定义与其示例 |
274	| Placeholder 残留 | XX.XX, TODO, [If...] 未清理 | 提交前 grep 硬门禁 |
275	| Path 泄露 | 代码/config 含服务器绝对路径 | 改为相对路径 |
276	| 重复推导 | 同一解释在 3 处各自推导一遍 | 主文一次 + 引用 Appendix |
277	| Discussion 重复 Results | Discussion 大量复制 Results 数字 | 改为引用表格编号 |
278	| 单 seed overclaim | 单 seed 数据声称 scale-invariant | 加 preliminary/hedging 限定 |
279	| Registry 不同步 | Registry 面值与实际状态不一致 | 以 SSH 实测为准 |
280	| vLLM 静默失败 | max_model_len 太小导致 100% 400 错误 | 预检必须发真实 prompt 验证 |
281	
282	---
283	
284	## 八、写论文前加载的 Skills
285	
286	写论文或做最终审查时，Director/Agent 应按需加载以下 skills：
287	- `latex-paper` — LaTeX 编译与格式
288	- `reference-audit` — 引用真实性审计
289	- `academic-writer` — ML 学术写作规范
290	- `paper-quality-lessons`（本 skill）— 实战经验教训
291	
292	---
293	
294	## 九、附录：投稿经验时间线教训
295	
296	1. **论文数字锁定时机**：最终编译前 24h 锁定所有数字，之后只允许格式修改
297	2. **GPU 不要空闲**：审稿期间如果 Agent 空闲，应该跑 ablation/扩展实验作为 rebuttal 储备
298	3. **匿名化提前做**：不要等到最后一天才检查路径泄露，fresh repo 需要时间清理
299	4. **9 页零余量策略**：NeurIPS 正文 9 页是硬限，每次新增内容必须等量压缩并编译验证
300	5. **Contingency 叙事提前写**：关键实验完成前就写好两版叙事，避免最后一刻仓促改稿
301	
````

</details>

实际由正向效应驱动时，必须加括注说明\n\n### 4.4 例子与定义必须一致\n\nTaxonomy 中的定义和对应示例不能自相矛盾。如果定义说 \"no new content\"，示例中就不能包含新信息。\n\n---\n\n## 五、投稿前检查清单（Pre-Submission Checklist）\n\n### 5.1 占位符扫描（硬门禁）\n\n提交前必须 grep 扫描，以下模式要求**零匹配**：\n```\nXX.XX, PLACEHOLDER, [If , TODO, TBD, \\placeholder{\n```\n所有条件占位段落（`[If ...]`）必须替换为最终文本。\n\n### 5.2 编译检查\n\n- [ ] 编译零 error\n- [ ] 无 `??` 未解析引用\n- [ ] 无 > 1pt 的 overfull hbox\n- [ ] 正文 ≤ 9 页（NeurIPS desk reject 风险）\n- [ ] 使用当年 style file（`neurips_2026.sty`）\n- [ ] Review 版启用行号\n- [ ] 匿名审稿版无作者姓名、无致谢\n\n### 5.3 数据完整性\n\n- [ ] 论文所有数字与 registry/provenance 交叉验证通过\n- [ ] 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）独立重算确认\n- [ ] 所有 evaluation protocol 差异已在论文中说明\n- [ ] 多 seed 结果报告均值和标准差\n- [ ] 单 seed 数据在 Limitations 中声明\n\n### 5.4 图表检查\n\n- [ ] 所有 figure PDF 存在且路径匹配\n- [ ] 图表不超栏宽（单栏 5.5in / 全宽 6.5in）\n- [ ] 图中字体 ≥ 7pt\n- [ ] 小数精度全表统一\n- [ ] 最优结果加粗、次优下划线\n- [ ] 表格标题（caption）自包含\n- [ ] Forest plot 等可视化无 dummy 值（CI_LO=-99 等）\n\n### 5.5 引用检查\n\n- [ ] 引用覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**（任一缺失说明调研不充分）\n- [ ] 引用密度与目标会议近年录用论文同量级\n- [ ] 引用键与 bib 条目 1:1 匹配\n- [ ] 无孤儿 bib 条目\n- [ ] 无幻觉引用（所有被引论文实际存在）\n- [ ] 近年论文的年份/会议准确\n- [ ] 审稿人要求的引用全部补齐\n\n### 5.6 匿名化检查\n\n- [ ] 源代码无硬编码绝对路径（`/Users/...`, `/root/...`）\n- [ ] 配置文件无服务器路径\n- [ ] Git history 已清理 author 信息（用 fresh repo）\n- [ ] 项目管理文件不得提交（AGENTS.md, memory.md, registry.yaml, task_context/ 等）\n- [ ] API Keys 通过环境变量，无泄露\n\n### 5.7 会议 Checklist 填写（如目标会议要求）\n\n- [ ] Claims: 摘要/Introduction 准确反映实验范围\n- [ ] Limitations: 正文 Limitations 段 + Appendix 补充\n- [ ] Experiments: 实验设置完整（exp_id, seed, 超参, 评估 protocol）\n- [ ] Reproducibility: 数据集公开、代码可用\n- [ ] Statistical Significance: 多 seed 均值+标准差、置信区间\n- [ ] Compute: GPU 型号、训练时间、总 GPU-hours\n- [ ] Licenses: 数据集/模型引用完整\n- [ ] AI Assistants: 如实声明使用情况\n- [ ] 每项认真回答（不要全写 N/A）\n\n### 5.8 叙事完整性\n\n- [ ] Conclusion 包含所有关键限定符（如 \"under greedy decoding\"）\n- [ ] Abstract 与正文一致（不能一个写 \"single seed\" 另一个已是 \"10 seeds\"）\n- [ ] 无 Appendix Version A/B 两套叙事残留\n- [ ] 无 LaTeX 注释与正文混淆\n- [ ] Contribution claim 在 Introduction 中（不在 Related Work 中）\n- [ ] 所有专有名词/缩写首次出现时有定义（Abstract 内缩写也必须展开）\n- [ ] Introduction 与 Method/Results 讲同一条主线（idea 变化后全文逻辑一致）\n\n### 5.9 去 AI 风格检查\n\n- [ ] **`grep \"—\" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 em-dash 必须全删，最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n- [ ] **`grep \" – \" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 en-dash 必须全删）\n- [ ] 无 \"-ing,\" 分词短语结尾（如 \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"）\n- [ ] 无连续 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"（同段最多 1 个）\n- [ ] 无 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"\n- [ ] 无 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\"\n- [ ] 自然段 5-8 句是正常的，不要每 2-3 句就换段\n\n### 5.10 模板与同类论文核对\n\n- [ ] LaTeX 模板从目标会议官网下载（不是他人转载、不是上一年）\n- [ ] `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项匹配 review / camera-ready 阶段\n- [ ] 写作前已参考 2-3 篇同领域近 1-2 年优秀论文，叙事节奏对齐\n\n---\n\n## 六、Reviewer 审查标准\n\n### 6.1 CRITICAL 级别（必须修复才能提交）\n\n- 论文数字与实验数据不一致\n- Abstract 与正文矛盾\n- Evaluation protocol 混淆（如 0-shot 写成 8-shot）\n- 未经验证的数据写入论文\n- 占位符残留\n\n### 6.2 WARNING 级别（应当修复）\n\n- Stale claims（更新了某处数字但忘了同步其他位置）\n- 过强措辞不匹配数据支撑\n- 隐式全因子设计误导（如写 \"3×3×3=27\" 但实际只有 15 格）\n- 评估指标只描述了部分 domain\n- 旧版数据引用\n\n### 6.3 数据一致性审查流程\n\n1. 从 registry 提取所有实验 metrics\n2. 对照论文中每一个内联数字（到行号级别）\n3. 独立重算所有衍生值\n4. 不一致处标注为 CRITICAL\n5. 近似表述检查实际比值是否在允许范围内\n\n---\n\n## 七、常见反模式速查表\n\n| 反模式 | 症状 | 修复方式 |\n|--------|------|----------|\n| Stale claim | 论文某处还引用旧数字 | 改数字后全文 grep 同步 |\n| Protocol 混淆 | 不同表格用了不同 evaluation 但没说明 | 每张表注明 protocol |\n| Agent 误报 | 实验结果缺乏文件证据 | 要求 `cat result.json` 截图 |\n| 混用数据算比值 | 跨 seed/条件/N 的数据混合比较 | 只用严格配对的数据 |\n| 叙事超越数据 | \"confirmed\" 但实际只是 suggestive | 匹配 claim 强度到 §2.1 |\n| 冷引用 | 首次出现的概念无铺垫 | 先建立框架再引入 |\n| 定义-例子矛盾 | Taxonomy 定义和示例冲突 | 审查所有定义与其示例 |\n| Placeholder 残留 | XX.XX, TODO, [If...] 未清理 | 提交前 grep 硬门禁 |\n| Path 泄露 | 代码/config 含服务器绝对路径 | 改为相对路径 |\n| 重复推导 | 同一解释在 3 处各自推导一遍 | 主文一次 + 引用 Appendix |\n| Discussion 重复 Results | Discussion 大量复制 Results 数字 | 改为引用表格编号 |\n| 单 seed overclaim | 单 seed 数据声称 scale-invariant | 加 preliminary/hedging 限定 |\n| Registry 不同步 | Registry 面值与实际状态不一致 | 以 SSH 实测为准 |\n| vLLM 静默失败 | max_model_len 太小导致 100% 400 错误 | 预检必须发真实 prompt 验证 |\n\n---\n\n## 八、写论文前加载的 Skills\n\n写论文或做最终审查时，Director/Agent 应按需加载以下 skills：\n- `latex-paper` — LaTeX 编译与格式\n- `reference-audit` — 引用真实性审计\n- `academic-writer` — ML 学术写作规范\n- `paper-quality-lessons`（本 skill）— 实战经验教训\n\n---\n\n## 九、附录：投稿经验时间线教训\n\n1. **论文数字锁定时机**：最终编译前 24h 锁定所有数字，之后只允许格式修改\n2. **GPU 不要空闲**：审稿期间如果 Agent 空闲，应该跑 ablation/扩展实验作为 rebuttal 储备\n3. **匿名化提前做**：不要等到最后一天才检查路径泄露，fresh repo 需要时间清理\n4. **9 页零余量策略**：NeurIPS 正文 9 页是硬限，每次新增内容必须等量压缩并编译验证\n5. **Contingency 叙事提前写**：关键实验完成前就写好两版叙事，避免最后一刻仓促改稿\n",

1	\begin{abstract}
2	When LLM-generated annotations feed into causal inference, annotation quality is the wrong thing to worry about; the causal DAG topology determines whether errors attenuate, amplify, or reverse the treatment effect estimate. We derive bias expressions for seven canonical topologies under $K$-class non-differential misclassification in linear structural equation models and show that $K {\geq} 3$ introduces a structural possibility of sign reversal absent in the binary case. Under proportional effects, five topologies preserve the treatment effect sign under regularity conditions; exposure and IV can reverse it. Under empirical LLM confusion matrices, sign reversal is confined to exposure topology. We introduce Annotation Sensitivity Values (ASVs), with proved ($K{=}2$, $K{=}3$ symmetric) and extensively validated (general $K{\geq}3$) monotonicity, yielding a vulnerability ranking from IV (maximally fragile) to M-bias (immune), and show that matrix-based corrections are provably ineffective under linear regression for four topologies due to Frisch--Waugh--Lovell invariance. A case study with 10 production LLM confusion matrices confirms: topology-aware correction reduces exposure bias from 61\% to $<$1\%, while topology-blind corrections are provably futile under linear regression for four topologies.
3	\end{abstract}
4

      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-prod_0510/projects/causal_annot_error_attrib/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
      "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nWhen LLM-generated annotations feed into causal inference, annotation quality is the wrong thing to worry about; the causal DAG topology determines whether errors attenuate, amplify, or reverse the treatment effect estimate. We derive bias expressions for seven canonical topologies under $K$-class non-differential misclassification in linear structural equation models and show that $K {\\geq} 3$ introduces a structural possibility of sign reversal absent in the binary case. Under proportional effects, five topologies preserve the treatment effect sign under regularity conditions; exposure and IV can reverse it. Under empirical LLM confusion matrices, sign reversal is confined to exposure topology. We introduce Annotation Sensitivity Values (ASVs), with proved ($K{=}2$, $K{=}3$ symmetric) and extensively validated (general $K{\\geq}3$) monotonicity, yielding a vulnerability ranking from IV (maximally fragile) to M-bias (immune), and show that matrix-based corrections are provably ineffective under linear regression for four topologies due to Frisch--Waugh--Lovell invariance. A case study with 10 production LLM confusion matrices confirms: topology-aware correction reduces exposure bias from 61\\% to $<$1\\%, while topology-blind corrections are provably futile under linear regression for four topologies.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
      "numLines": 4,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 4
    }
  },
  "sourceToolAssistantUUID": "00d1e63a-c12d-4c43-8429-be38279f746b",

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Large language models are rapidly replacing human annotators in computational social science~\cite{gilardi2023chatgpt, ziems2024can, egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023, baumann2025}. Most tasks are inherently multi-class (stance detection: 3--4 categories, sentiment: 5, disaster response: 10+), and annotation errors propagate into downstream causal estimates in ways that depend on both error structure and causal design. \citet{baumann2025} show that 31\% of hypotheses yield incorrect conclusions with state-of-the-art LLMs, with 60\% error correlation across models~\cite{kim_garg2025}. Standard metrics do not capture this risk: within $F_1$ 0.61--0.69, causal bias varies by an order of magnitude across topologies.
5	
6	The transition at $K{\geq}3$ is not gradual but structural. For $K{=}2$, the stochastic constraint forces any confusion matrix with positive diagonal to preserve coefficient signs, an algebraic property of the $2{\times}2$ matrix dimension~\cite{brenner1993}. This guarantee vanishes at $K{\geq}3$: the higher-dimensional confusion matrix admits negative effective weights on categories with opposite-sign coefficients, enabling sign reversal even under high accuracy~\cite{veirod_laake2001}. Under a uniform Dirichlet prior, 83.4\% of $K{=}3$ exposure confusion matrices produce sign reversal of at least one secondary coefficient; under realistic accuracy (diagonal $\geq 0.7$), 2.1\% still do. The primary treatment coefficient is never reversed under the tested $\beta$ configurations where a single category dominates ($\beta_1 \gg \beta_2$), an empirical regularity we discuss in Section~\ref{sec:method}.
7	
8	$F_1$ does not exhibit significant rank correlation with causal bias ($\rho = -0.10$, $p = 0.45$ across 56 HumAID scenarios; bias 4.1--42.1\% within $F_1$ 0.614--0.690). Prior work noted polytomous sign reversal~\cite{dosemeci1990, veirod_laake2001} and confounder amplification~\cite{brenner1993}, but covered at most one topology~\cite{lee_wood_doughty2024}. We present the first systematic analysis jointly addressing multi-class ($K{\geq}3$) misclassification across seven DAG topologies, with analytical predictions matching Monte Carlo within 1.6\% MAPE across 161 scenarios.
9	
10	We address these gaps with three contributions:
11	
12	\begin{enumerate}
13	\item \textbf{Multi-class bias taxonomy.} We derive bias expressions for seven canonical DAG topologies\footnote{These span the four structural roles of the annotated variable: treatment (exposure), outcome-side covariate (confounding, mediation, M-bias, front-door), selection variable (collider), and instrument (IV).} (Figure~\ref{fig:dag_taxonomy}) under $K$-class non-differential misclassification, establishing a sign-preservation/reversal dichotomy: five topologies preserve the treatment effect sign, while exposure and IV can reverse it (binarization amplifies this gap $>$150${\times}$, from $\bar{\Delta}{=}0.006$ for M-bias to $0.926$ for IV; Appendix~\ref{app:binarization}).
14	
15	\item \textbf{Annotation Sensitivity Values.} Building on the bias taxonomy, we define three ASV diagnostics (magnitude, significance, and sign-flip) with proved envelope monotonicity for $K{=}2$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:monotone}) and $K{=}3$ symmetric confusion matrices (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3_symmetric}), with $\varepsilon$-approximate monotonicity for general $K{\geq}3$ ($\varepsilon < 0.017$ across 545{,}945 adversarial curves spanning four structural families; Conjecture~\ref{conj:monotone}). These reveal a vulnerability spectrum from IV (maximally fragile) to M-bias (immune, ASV $= \infty$), enabling topology-specific error tolerance assessment.
16	
17	\item \textbf{Topology-aware correction routing.} We observe that FWL invariance renders matrix-based corrections provably ineffective under linear regression for four covariate topologies, and evaluate five methods across 210 configurations (PPI++ harms M-bias ($-88\%$), MC-SIMEX degrades front-door ($-62.4\%$)) and provide a topology-aware decision procedure (Algorithm~\ref{alg:correction}, Appendix), validated end-to-end with production LLM confusion matrices from VAST stance detection (Appendix~\ref{sec:real_cm_demo}).
18	\end{enumerate}
19	
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Measurement error in causal inference.}
5	Foundations appear in \citet{carroll2006, gustafson2004, buonaccorsi2010, bound2001measurement}, with DAG-based formalization by \citet{hernan_cole2009} and \citet{kuroki_pearl2014}. For binary variables, non-differential misclassification biases toward the null~\cite{bross1954misclassification, brenner1993, ogburn_vanderweele2012}, but this fails for polytomous exposures~\cite{dosemeci1990, jurek2005, veirod_laake2001}. Extensions address differential error~\cite{edwards_lessler_naimi2021, imai_yamamoto2010}, mismeasured covariates~\cite{shu_yi2019a, shu_yi2019b}, mediation~\cite{valeri_vanderweele2013}, and nonclassical error~\cite{schennach2020mismeasured, nguimkeu_denteh_tchernis2019, wei2025multiply}. All analyze a single DAG; ours characterizes how the same confusion matrix produces qualitatively different bias across seven topologies.
6	
7	\paragraph{Text classifiers and LLM annotation.}
8	\citet{grimmer2022} and \citet{feder_etal2022} provide foundations for text-as-data causal inference. \citet{wood_doughty2018} first applied DAG reasoning to classifier error; \citet{teblunthuis2024} gave the most complete binary treatment ($K{=}2$, four DAGs); \citet{lee_wood_doughty2024} extended to categorical LLM classifiers but covered only confounding. On annotation quality, \citet{gilardi2023chatgpt} and \citet{ziems2024can} document rapid LLM adoption~\cite{tan2024llm}, \citet{baumann2025} found 31\% incorrect conclusions, and \citet{kim_garg2025} showed 60\% error correlation~\cite{pangakis_wolken_fasching2023, cheng_mayya_sedoc2024, audinet2025, keith_jensen_oconnor2020, variance_aware_llm2026, hidden_measurement_error2026}. These works either restrict to binary labels, cover a single topology, or diagnose quality without connecting it to causal validity.
9	
10	\paragraph{Sensitivity analysis and correction methods.}
11	Our ASV framework draws on E-values~\cite{vanderweele_ding2017, mathur2018}, robustness values~\cite{cinelli2020making}, and mediation sensitivity~\cite{imai_keele_yamamoto2010, ding_vanderweele2016, pryzant_etal2021}, but parametrizes annotation error severity rather than omitted variable strength. \citet{lash2009}, \citet{blackwell_honaker_king2017}, \citet{mahajan2006}, and \citet{hu2008} provide complementary frameworks for misclassified regressors. For correction, PPI++~\cite{angelopoulos2023} and DSL~\cite{egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023} are topology-agnostic~\cite{zrnic_candes2024, demirel2024prediction, cadei2025, gligoric2025}. We show this agnosticism is costly: correction effectiveness is topology-dependent, with matrix-based methods provably ineffective under FWL decomposition for four topologies.
12	
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup and Notation}
5	\label{sec:notation}
6	
7	Let $A$ denote a true $K$-class categorical variable (e.g., sentiment with $K{=}3$ levels) and $A^*$ its observed, potentially misclassified version produced by an LLM annotator. The misclassification process is characterized by a $K{\times}K$ confusion matrix $C$ with entries $C_{jk} = P(A^*{=}j \mid A{=}k)$, where each column sums to one (column-stochastic: each column gives the distribution of predicted labels for a given true label). Following \citet{hernan_cole2009}, we represent this measurement process using \emph{starred-node} DAGs: the true variable $A$ is latent, and the observed $A^*$ is connected to $A$ via a dashed measurement edge.
8	
9	\paragraph{Notation conventions.}
10	Our analysis characterizes probability limits ($N \to \infty$) under the FWL theorem~\cite{frisch1933partial}, which explains why matrix-based corrections fail for covariate topologies (Section~\ref{sec:correction}). Notation is collected in Table~\ref{tab:notation} (Appendix~\ref{app:notation_table}).
11	
12	\begin{definition}[Non-differential misclassification]
13	\label{def:nondiff}
14	Misclassification of $A$ is non-differential with respect to outcome $Y$ and covariates $\mathbf{Z}$ if $P(A^*{=}j \mid A{=}k, Y, \mathbf{Z}) = P(A^*{=}j \mid A{=}k) = C_{jk}$ for all $j,k$.
15	\end{definition}
16	
17	In batch annotation pipelines, the LLM processes each text independently without access to $Y$, making non-differentiality an architectural invariant~\cite{ziems2024can}; Y-stratified permutation tests confirm this (1/12 rejection at $\alpha{=}0.05$; Appendix~\ref{sec:y_stratified_test}). Non-differentiality may be violated when $Y$ correlates with text content, in which case our Y-stratified test provides empirical verification. Under stress testing, sign reversal occurs in 14/16 $K{\geq}3$ exposure configurations at $\eta{=}0.01$ structured departure (Appendix~\ref{sec:nondiff_stress}).
18	
19	Throughout, $\tau$ denotes the causal estimand of interest, whose definition varies by topology: the confounder's coefficient, the treatment effect, the indirect effect, or the Wald ratio (Appendix~\ref{app:confounding}ff.).
20	
21	We study seven canonical DAG topologies (Figure~\ref{fig:dag_taxonomy})~\cite{pearl2009, hernan2020causal}, deriving probability limits under linear SEMs~\cite{carroll2006}; logistic regression robustness is verified in Appendix~\ref{sec:logistic_dgp} and~\ref{sec:fwl_nonlinear}.
22	
23	\subsection{Multi-Class Bias Expressions}
24	\label{sec:bias_expressions}
25	
26	For each topology, we derive the $\operatorname{plim}$\footnote{The probability limit (plim) is the value to which an estimator converges as sample size $N \to \infty$; it characterizes systematic bias separately from sampling variability.} of the naive estimator that substitutes $A^*$ for $A$. Let $\tau$ denote the target causal parameter, whose precise definition is topology-specific (e.g., the coefficient on $X$ for confounding, the category-specific $\beta_j$ for exposure, or the direct effect $\tau_{\text{direct}}$ for mediation), and $\hat{\tau}(A^*)$ the estimator using misclassified annotations.
27	
28	\paragraph{Confounding ($A$ = confounder).}
29	When $A$ confounds the $X{\to}Y$ relationship, the OLS estimator controlling for $A^*$ has probability limit
30	\begin{equation}
31	\label{eq:confounding}
32	\operatorname{plim}\;\hat{\tau}(A^*) = \bigl(\mathbb{E}[Z^{\prime}Z]\bigr)^{-1}\mathbb{E}[Z^{\prime}X]\,\boldsymbol{\beta},
33	\end{equation}
34	where $Z = [X, \text{dummies}(A^*)]$, $\boldsymbol{\beta}$ contains the true coefficients, and the target parameter $\tau$ is the coefficient on $X$. Misclassification produces residual confounding that monotonically approaches the omitted variable bias, but the sign of $\hat{\tau}$ is preserved: $\tau_{\text{sign\_flip}} = 0$ structurally.\footnote{Consistent with \citet{brenner1993}: misclassification degrades confounding control, producing amplification ($|\hat{\tau}| > |\tau|$, universal in this topology) but never sign reversal ($\operatorname{sign}(\hat{\tau}) \neq \operatorname{sign}(\tau)$).}
35	
36	\paragraph{Exposure ($A$ = treatment variable).}
37	When $A$ is the exposure of interest, the biased coefficient for category $j$ becomes a weighted combination of true coefficients:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:exposure}
40	\operatorname{plim}\;\hat{\beta}_j(A^*) = \sum_{k=1}^{K} w_{jk}\,\beta_k,
41	\end{equation}
42	where $w_{jk}$ depends on the confusion matrix $C$ and the population marginal $\pi_k = P(A{=}k)$. Unlike binary misclassification~\cite{brenner1993}, $K{\geq}3$ permits $w_{jk}$ to assign negative weight to categories with opposite-sign coefficients, producing \emph{sign reversal} (83.4\% under uniform Dirichlet prior); this is structurally impossible at $K{=}2$.
43	
44	\begin{figure*}[t]
45	\centering
46	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig2_bias_distribution.pdf}
47	\caption{Bias distributions under 100{,}000 random $K{=}3$ confusion matrices (uniform Dirichlet; realistic-accuracy results in Section~\ref{sec:taxonomy_results}). Red = sign reversal. Exposure: 83.4\% sign reversal; IV: 52.6\%.}
48	\label{fig:bias_distribution}
49	\end{figure*}
50	
51	\paragraph{Numerical Verification 2: Primary coefficient preservation.}
52	Across 23 empirical LLM configurations, the primary coefficient (the category with the largest true effect $|\beta_k|$) never reverses sign, while 61\% reverse a secondary (non-dominant) coefficient, confirmed under 100{,}000 Dirichlet matrices with diagonal ${\geq}\,0.7$ across six beta-ratio conditions (1.0 to 50.0) with 0\% primary sign-flip (Appendix~\ref{app:exposure}).
53	
54	\paragraph{Mediation ($A$ = mediator).}
55	When $A$ mediates the $X{\to}Y$ relationship~\cite{vanderweele2015explanation}, the direct effect estimator controlling for $A^*$ has probability limit
56	\begin{equation}
57	\label{eq:mediation}
58	\operatorname{plim}\;\hat{\tau}_{\text{direct}}(A^*) = \tau_{\text{direct}} + \bigl(1 - \lambda(C)\bigr)\,\tau_{\text{indirect}},
59	\end{equation}
60	where $\lambda(C) \in [0,1]$ under proportional effects (Appendix~\ref{app:proof}, Proposition~\ref{prop:lambda_bound}), with $\lambda(I) = 1$ under perfect classification. Misclassification ($\lambda < 1$) causes the residual indirect effect to be absorbed into the direct path estimate, producing upward bias in $|\hat{\tau}_{\text{direct}}|$ but preserving the sign ($\tau_{\text{sign\_flip}} = 0$).
61	
62	\paragraph{Collider, M-bias, and front-door.}
63	Three topologies exhibit protective or immune behavior. \textbf{Collider:} conditioning on $A^*$ instead of $A$ attenuates the spurious path, so annotation error reduces collider bias ($\tau_{\text{sign\_flip}} = 0$). \textbf{M-bias:} in the M-shaped DAG~\cite{greenland2003}, misclassifying $A$ weakens the M-bias path; this topology is immune ($\text{ASV} = \infty$). \textbf{Front-door:} the front-door estimator~\cite{bellemare_bloem2024} is attenuated in both $X \to A$ and $A \to Y$ stages, producing moderate bias with $\tau_{\text{sign\_flip}} = 0$.
64	
65	\paragraph{Instrumental variable ($A$ = instrument).}
66	The Wald estimator~\cite{angrist_imbens1995} $\hat{\tau}_{\text{IV}} = \hat{\text{cov}}(A^*, Y) / \hat{\text{cov}}(A^*, X)$ has $C$ in the denominator. Misclassification can drive first-stage coefficients toward zero or reverse their sign; under random Dirichlet matrices, 52.6\% produce sign reversal, making IV the most fragile topology (Appendix~\ref{sec:iv_dirichlet_subspace}).
67	
68	\paragraph{Structural sign-flip guarantee.}
69	\begin{proposition}[Sign preservation: analytic results]
70	\label{prop:signflip}
71	Under non-differential misclassification with $C_{kk} > 0$ for all $k$:
72	\emph{(a)}~Confounding preserves sign when $\operatorname{sign}(\tau) = \operatorname{sign}(\tau {+} B_{\mathrm{OV}})$, where $B_{\mathrm{OV}} {=} \operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))/\operatorname{Var}(T)$ is the omitted-variable bias (Cauchy--Schwarz; Appendix~\ref{app:confounding}).
73	\emph{(b)}~Collider preserves sign when $|\tau| > \sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g)\operatorname{Var}(h)}/\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T{\mid}A)]$ (Berkson attenuation; Appendix~\ref{app:collider}); empirically verified across 100{,}000 matrices with zero violations.
74	\emph{(c)}~Exposure can produce sign reversal for $K {\geq} 3$, even with $C_{kk} {>} 0.5$ (counterexample in Appendix~\ref{app:exposure}).
75	\end{proposition}
76	
77	Sign preservation for non-exposure topologies holds under topology-specific conditions (Table~\ref{tab:sign_conditions}, Appendix~\ref{app:conditions}), verified across 100{,}000 random matrices with zero violations; analytic proofs for general $K$ remain open.
78	
79	\subsection{Annotation Sensitivity Values}
80	\label{sec:asv}
81	
82	We adapt the sensitivity analysis paradigm (E-values~\cite{vanderweele_ding2017}, partial-$R^2$ bounds~\cite{cinelli2020making}, coefficient stability~\cite{oster2019}) to $K{\times}K$ annotation error, defining Annotation Sensitivity Values (ASVs): we prove $\varepsilon$-approximate monotonicity along $C(\delta)$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:monotone}), reducing $K^2$ dimensions to a scalar; define three ASV diagnostics for topology-dependent failure modes; and combine ASVs with FWL invariance to route corrections.
83	
84	\paragraph{Parameterization.}
85	We set $C(\delta) = (1{-}\delta)I + \delta C_0$, where $C_0$ is a reference confusion matrix and $\delta \in [0,1]$ controls severity ($\delta{=}0$: perfect; $\delta{=}1$: matches $C_0$). This linear path is one of many in the column-stochastic polytope from $I$ to $C_0$; ASV thresholds are path-dependent, but our bootstrap procedure (Section~\ref{sec:asv_results}) captures estimation uncertainty along the empirical path. We recommend bootstrapping $C_0$ and reporting ASV confidence intervals (Appendix~\ref{sec:bootstrap_asv}).
86	\paragraph{Three ASV types.}
87	We define three complementary measures: (1)~\textbf{Magnitude ASV} $\delta^*_{\text{mag}} = \min\{\delta : |\operatorname{plim}\;\hat{\tau}(C(\delta)) - \tau| > \epsilon\}$, analogous to E-values; (2)~\textbf{Significance ASV} $\delta^*_{\text{sig}} = \min\{\delta : |\operatorname{plim}\;\hat{\tau}(C(\delta))| < z_{\alpha/2} \cdot SE(N)\}$, relevant for attenuating topologies; (3)~\textbf{Sign-flip ASV} $\delta^*_{\text{sf}} = \min\{\delta : \operatorname{sign}(\hat{\tau}) \neq \operatorname{sign}(\tau)\}$, for exposure and IV only. The significance ASV depends on $N$ through $SE(N)$, exhibiting a sharp phase transition at a critical $N^*$ below which all rejections are blocked.
88	
89	\begin{theorem}[Envelope monotonicity, $K{=}2$]
90	\label{thm:monotone}
91	For $K{=}2$ confounding and mediation topologies with proportional effects ($f = \lambda g$ for some scalar $\lambda$) under $C(\delta) = (1{-}\delta)I + \delta C_0$ with $(C_0)_{jj} \geq \tfrac{1}{2}$, $|B(C(\delta))|$ is monotonically non-decreasing in $\delta \in [0,1]$.
92	\end{theorem}
93	
94	The proof (Appendix~\ref{app:monotonicity_proof},~\ref{par:med_monotone}) exploits perspective function convexity. Proportional effects ($f{=}\lambda g$) is plausible when overall annotation competence drives both paths; all 23 empirical configurations preserve sign, with 97.3\% under non-proportional effects (Appendix~\ref{sec:proportional_effects}).
95	
96	\begin{theorem}[$K{=}3$ symmetric monotonicity]
97	\label{thm:k3_symmetric}
98	For $K{=}3$ with symmetric confusion matrix $C_{jk} = c$ for $j \neq k$ and $C_{kk} = 1{-}2c$, $c \in (0, 1/3)$, and proportional effects ($f{=}\lambda g$), envelope monotonicity holds exactly: $|B(C(\delta))|$ is strictly monotone in $\delta$ for confounding, mediation, and collider topologies whenever $\mathrm{Var}(g) > 0$.
99	\end{theorem}
100	
101	Theorem~\ref{thm:k3_symmetric} anchors exact monotonicity at $K{=}3$; for general asymmetric matrices ($d_{\mathrm{rel}}{=}0.33$; Remark~\ref{rem:symmetry}), Conjecture~\ref{conj:monotone} provides $\varepsilon < 0.017$ coverage. The proof exploits the reduction $s = c\delta$ (Appendix~\ref{app:k3_symmetric_proof}).
102	
103	\paragraph{From proofs to numerical evidence.}
104	Exact monotonicity (Theorems~\ref{thm:monotone}--\ref{thm:k3_symmetric}) does not extend to general $K{\geq}3$ column-stochastic structures; we state a conjecture supported by numerical evidence:
105	
106	\begin{conjecture}[$\varepsilon$-approximate envelope monotonicity]
107	\label{conj:monotone}
108	For general $K{\geq}3$ non-IV topologies under $C(\delta) = (1{-}\delta)I + \delta C_0$, $|B(C(\delta))|$ is $\varepsilon$-approximately monotonic with $\varepsilon < 0.02$ for $|\beta| \leq 1$, scaling with effect magnitude (Table~\ref{tab:conjecture_verify},~\ref{tab:dgp_sensitivity}; Appendix~\ref{sec:conjecture_verify}). The safe/fragile partition (five sign-preserving topologies versus exposure and IV) is robust across all tested parameterizations.
109	\end{conjecture}
110	
111	Numerical support spans 100{,}000 Dirichlet matrices and 545{,}945 adversarial curves (Appendix~\ref{app:adversarial_monotonicity}).
112	
113	\begin{remark}[ASV and E-values as complementary tools]
114	\label{rem:evalue}
115	ASV and E-values~\cite{vanderweele_ding2017} address orthogonal sources (annotation error vs.\ unmeasured confounding) and are most valuable jointly (Table~\ref{tab:sensitivity_comparison}, Appendix). ASV is topology-dependent and targets sign-flip failure modes absent from confounding analysis.
116	\end{remark}
117	
118	Under alternating-sign effects ($\boldsymbol{\beta}{=}[0,\,1.0,\,-0.5]$), magnitude ASV ranges from 0.09 (exposure) to $\infty$ (M-bias), a $4{\times}$ difference determined by topology (Table~\ref{tab:asv_stability}). Sign-flip ASV $< 1$ flags sign-reversal-prone pairs; magnitude ASV $< \delta_{\text{observed}}$ indicates bias exceeds tolerance (Algorithm~\ref{alg:correction}); the safe/fragile partition is stable across parameterizations ($\varepsilon < 0.02$; Appendix~\ref{sec:asv_stability}).
119

The significance ASV depends on $N$ through $SE(N)$, exhibiting a sharp phase transition at a critical $N^*$ below which all rejections are blocked.\n\n\\begin{theorem}[Envelope monotonicity, $K{=}2$]\n\\label{thm:monotone}\nFor $K{=}2$ confounding and mediation topologies with proportional effects ($f = \\lambda g$ for some scalar $\\lambda$) under $C(\\delta) = (1{-}\\delta)I + \\delta C_0$ with $(C_0)_{jj} \\geq \\tfrac{1}{2}$, $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is monotonically non-decreasing in $\\delta \\in [0,1]$.\n\\end{theorem}\n\nThe proof (Appendix~\\ref{app:monotonicity_proof},~\\ref{par:med_monotone}) exploits perspective function convexity. Proportional effects ($f{=}\\lambda g$) is plausible when overall annotation competence drives both paths; all 23 empirical configurations preserve sign, with 97.3\\% under non-proportional effects (Appendix~\\ref{sec:proportional_effects}).\n\n\\begin{theorem}[$K{=}3$ symmetric monotonicity]\n\\label{thm:k3_symmetric}\nFor $K{=}3$ with symmetric confusion matrix $C_{jk} = c$ for $j \\neq k$ and $C_{kk} = 1{-}2c$, $c \\in (0, 1/3)$, and proportional effects ($f{=}\\lambda g$), envelope monotonicity holds exactly: $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is strictly monotone in $\\delta$ for confounding, mediation, and collider topologies whenever $\\mathrm{Var}(g) > 0$.\n\\end{theorem}\n\nTheorem~\\ref{thm:k3_symmetric} anchors exact monotonicity at $K{=}3$; for general asymmetric matrices ($d_{\\mathrm{rel}}{=}0.33$; Remark~\\ref{rem:symmetry}), Conjecture~\\ref{conj:monotone} provides $\\varepsilon < 0.017$ coverage. The proof exploits the reduction $s = c\\delta$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:k3_symmetric_proof}).\n\n\\paragraph{From proofs to numerical evidence.}\nExact monotonicity (Theorems~\\ref{thm:monotone}--\\ref{thm:k3_symmetric}) does not extend to general $K{\\geq}3$ column-stochastic structures; we state a conjecture supported by numerical evidence:\n\n\\begin{conjecture}[$\\varepsilon$-approximate envelope monotonicity]\n\\label{conj:monotone}\nFor general $K{\\geq}3$ non-IV topologies under $C(\\delta) = (1{-}\\delta)I + \\delta C_0$, $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is $\\varepsilon$-approximately monotonic with $\\varepsilon < 0.02$ for $|\\beta| \\leq 1$, scaling with effect magnitude (Table~\\ref{tab:conjecture_verify},~\\ref{tab:dgp_sensitivity}; Appendix~\\ref{sec:conjecture_verify}). The safe/fragile partition (five sign-preserving topologies versus exposure and IV) is robust across all tested parameterizations.\n\\end{conjecture}\n\nNumerical support spans 100{,}000 Dirichlet matrices and 545{,}945 adversarial curves (Appendix~\\ref{app:adversarial_monotonicity}).\n\n\\begin{remark}[ASV and E-values as complementary tools]\n\\label{rem:evalue}\nASV and E-values~\\cite{vanderweele_ding2017} address orthogonal sources (annotation error vs.\\ unmeasured confounding) and are most valuable jointly (Table~\\ref{tab:sensitivity_comparison}, Appendix). ASV is topology-dependent and targets sign-flip failure modes absent from confounding analysis.\n\\end{remark}\n\nUnder alternating-sign effects ($\\boldsymbol{\\beta}{=}[0,\\,1.0,\\,-0.5]$), magnitude ASV ranges from 0.09 (exposure) to $\\infty$ (M-bias), a $4{\\times}$ difference determined by topology (Table~\\ref{tab:asv_stability}). Sign-flip ASV $< 1$ flags sign-reversal-prone pairs; magnitude ASV $< \\delta_{\\text{observed}}$ indicates bias exceeds tolerance (Algorithm~\\ref{alg:correction}); the safe/fragile partition is stable across parameterizations ($\\varepsilon < 0.02$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:asv_stability}).\n",

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We validate through Monte Carlo simulation ($K{=}3$), cross-$K$ robustness checks ($K{=}5,7$), and empirical validation with real LLM confusion matrices from three NLP tasks ($K{=}2,3,10$).
5	
6	\subsection{Setup}
7	\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	For each topology, we sample 100{,}000 confusion matrices from a Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ prior, compute analytical probability limits, and validate against Monte Carlo simulation ($N{=}10{,}000$). ASV experiments use $C(\delta) = (1{-}\delta)I + \delta C_0$.
10	
11	\subsection{Bias Taxonomy}
12	\label{sec:taxonomy_results}
13	
14	\paragraph{Theory-MC agreement.}
15	Analytical probability limits match Monte Carlo estimates with MAPE of 1.6\%, averaged across 161 scenarios (23 LLM configurations $\times$ 7 topologies; per-topology range: 0.3\%--3.7\%).
16	
17	\paragraph{Exposure DAG: sign reversal under multi-class misclassification.}
18	Under realistic accuracy (diagonal $\geq 0.7$), the violation rate is 13.7\% with 2.1\% sign reversal; at diagonal $\geq 0.9$, violation persists at 11.9\% (Appendix~\ref{sec:iv_dirichlet_subspace}). Under the full Dirichlet prior, 85\% of matrices violate the binary ``toward-null'' guarantee and 83.4\% cause sign reversal; $\min_k C_{kk} < 0.55$ predicts sign reversal with 91\% accuracy (Appendix~\ref{sec:sign_determinants}). The $K{=}2$ control confirms classical attenuation~\cite{brenner1993}, establishing the multi-class failure as structurally distinct.
19	
20	\paragraph{Structural guarantee for non-exposure topologies.}
21	Five topologies exhibit zero sign-flip rate (Table~\ref{tab:taxonomy}): confounding, mediation, and front-door show 100\% amplification~\cite{brenner1993, dosemeci1990} but never reverse the sign; collider is protective ($|B(C)|$ non-increasing; Conjecture~\ref{conj:monotone}); M-bias shows weakest sensitivity (53.2\% violation). All patterns persist at $K{=}5,7$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:cross_k_robustness}).
22	
23	\paragraph{IV: Wald estimator instability.}
24	The IV topology is the sole non-exposure exception: 52.6\% of random matrices cause sign reversal, stable across $K{=}3,5,7$. Diagonal dominance ($C_{kk} > 0.5$) eliminates sign reversal but preserves amplification (Appendix~\ref{sec:iv_dirichlet_subspace}); however, all 23 empirical LLM configurations show zero IV sign reversal.
25	
26	\begin{table}[t]
27	\centering
28	\caption{Bias under $K{=}3$ non-differential misclassification (100{,}000 Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ matrices). Violation: $|\text{bias}| > 0$; Sign Flip: any coefficient reverses sign; Amplif.: $|\hat{\tau}| > |\tau|$. $\tau_{\text{sf}}{=}0$?: sign-flip guarantee holds unconditionally (Yes), conditionally (Cond.; Proposition~\ref{prop:signflip}), or not (No). Collider 100\% amplification reflects Berkson bias; misclassification is protective.}
29	\label{tab:taxonomy}
30	\small
31	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
32	\toprule
33	\textbf{Topology} & \textbf{Violation} & \textbf{Sign Flip} & \textbf{Amplif.} & $\tau_{\text{sf}}{=}0$? \\
34	\midrule
35	Confounding & 100\% & 0\% & 100\% & Cond. \\
36	Exposure & 85\% & 83.4\% & 14.1\% & No \\
37	Mediation & 100\% & 0\% & 100\% & Cond. \\
38	Front-door & 100\% & 0\% & 100\% & Cond. \\
39	Collider & 100\% & 0\% & 100\% & Cond. \\
40	M-bias & 53.2\% & 0\% & 10.4\% & Yes \\
41	IV & 100\% & 52.6\% & 100\% & No \\
42	\bottomrule
43	\end{tabular}}
44	\end{table}
45	
46	The bias distributions are visualized in Figure~\ref{fig:bias_distribution}.
47	
48	\subsection{ASV Framework Validation}
49	\label{sec:asv_results}
50	
51	\begin{figure*}[t]
52	\centering
53	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig3_asv_ranking.pdf}
54	\caption{ASV vulnerability ranking across topologies. Three ASV types (magnitude, significance, sign-flip) reveal a topology-dependent spectrum from IV (maximally fragile) to M-bias (immune, ASV $= \infty$).}
55	\label{fig:asv_ranking}
56	\end{figure*}
57	
58	\begin{table}[t]
59	\centering
60	\small
61	\caption{ASV summary across topologies ($K{=}3$, $\tau{=}0.5$). ---: not applicable.}
62	\label{tab:asv_summary}
63	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
64	\toprule
65	\textbf{Topology} & \textbf{Mag ASV} & \textbf{Power} & \textbf{Sign-flip} \\
66	\midrule
67	IV          & 0.10--0.61 & 0\%   & 1.1--6.7 \\
68	Front-door  & 0.06--0.39 & 100\% & --- \\
69	Confounding & 0.08--0.49 & 100\% & --- \\
70	Mediation   & 0.08--0.45 & 0\%   & --- \\
71	Collider    & 0.34--2.03 & 0\%   & --- \\
72	M-bias      & $\infty$   & 98\%  & --- \\
73	Exposure    & ---        & ---   & 83.4\% \\
74	\bottomrule
75	\end{tabular}
76	\end{table}
77	
78	\paragraph{ASV results.}
79	The ASV envelope is $\varepsilon$-approximately monotonic for non-IV topologies ($\varepsilon < 0.02$ for non-exposure under moderate effects, larger for exposure; Conjecture~\ref{conj:monotone}). Figure~\ref{fig:asv_ranking} shows the vulnerability ranking: IV is maximally fragile, M-bias is immune ($\text{ASV} = \infty$). Sign-flip ASV: 83.4\% of exposure matrices yield $\delta^*_{\text{sf}} < 1$; IV ranges 1.1--6.7 (Table~\ref{tab:asv_summary}). Significance ASV exhibits a phase transition at $N^* \approx 6{,}000$--$10{,}000$; bootstrap CIs ($B{=}1{,}000$; Appendix~\ref{sec:bootstrap_asv}) confirm stable topology ranking despite wide IV intervals.
80	
81	\subsection{Empirical Validation}
82	\label{sec:empirical}
83	
84	We validate using real LLM confusion matrices from HumAID~\cite{alam2021humaid} ($K{=}10$), VAST ($K{=}3$), and CivilComments ($K{=}2$): four LLMs, 23 configurations, 161 bias scenarios.
85	
86	\paragraph{Empirical bias ranking.}
87	HumAID ($K{=}10$) matches predictions: IV (12.4--42.1\%) and exposure (9.0--17.4\%) dominate; remaining topologies $<$15\%. $F_1$ does not exhibit significant rank correlation with bias ($\rho = -0.10$, $p = 0.45$; Figure~\ref{fig:f1_vs_bias}), but per-topology patterns diverge ($\rho \leq -0.53$ for five topologies vs.\ $0.87$ for M-bias; Appendix~\ref{sec:within_topo_f1}). Of exposure coefficients, 14/23 (61\%) exhibit sign reversal, all secondary; zero across 138 non-exposure scenarios.
88	
89	\begin{figure*}[t]
90	\centering
91	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig5_f1_vs_bias.pdf}
92	\caption{Topology determines downstream causal bias, not $F_1$. HumAID $K{=}10$ (56 points): within $F_1$ range 0.614--0.690, bias varies $10{\times}$ (IV 42.1\% vs.\ collider $<$5.6\%).}
93	\label{fig:f1_vs_bias}
94	\end{figure*}
95	
96	
97	\subsection{Correction Guidance}
98	\label{sec:correction}
99	
100	We evaluate five correction methods (naive plug-in, MLA, DSL~\cite{egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023}, MC-SIMEX~\cite{kuchenhoff2006simex}, PPI++~\cite{angelopoulos2023}) across all topologies (210 configurations; Table~\ref{tab:correction}, Appendix~\ref{app:supplementary}). Regression calibration~\cite{carroll2006} is designed for continuous measurement error and does not apply to categorical misclassification.
101	
102	\paragraph{FWL invariance.}
103	Under linear regression, FWL invariance renders matrix-based methods (naive plug-in, MLA) provably ineffective for covariate topologies, achieving 0\% bias reduction (Appendix~\ref{app:fwl_proof}); under logistic regression this breaks but ranking persists ($|\Delta| < 0.02$; Appendix~\ref{sec:fwl_nonlinear}). To our knowledge, this futility result (that matrix-based corrections cannot reduce covariate-induced bias under FWL decomposition) has not been explicitly stated in the measurement error literature~\cite{carroll2006, buonaccorsi2010}.
104	
105	\paragraph{Method ranking.}
106	PPI++ achieves the highest RMSE reduction (47.0\% at $K{=}3$) but requires gold labels and harms M-bias ($-$88\%); MC-SIMEX is gold-free and $K$-robust (27--70\%), though it harms front-door ($-$62.4\%); rankings are stable across $K$ ($\rho \geq 0.80$; Appendix~\ref{sec:correction_k_sensitivity}).
107	
108	\paragraph{Correction decision procedure.}
109	Algorithm~\ref{alg:correction} (Appendix) routes through three stages: risk assessment (exposure/IV high; M-bias low), method selection (PPI++ with gold, MC-SIMEX/DSL without), and FWL screening (skip plug-in/MLA for covariate topologies). Under topology uncertainty, ASV under candidate topologies provides worst-case sensitivity.
110	
111	\paragraph{Case studies and sensitivity.}
112	ClinicalBERT and HumAID ($K{=}10$) confirm all patterns under linear and logistic models ($|\Delta| < 0.02$; Appendix~\ref{app:case_study},~\ref{sec:correction_k_sensitivity}). End-to-end demos (Table~\ref{tab:e2e-demo}; Appendix~\ref{sec:real_cm_demo}) validate: PPI++ reduces exposure bias from 42.9\% to 0.2\%, confounding achieves 0\% improvement (FWL), and topology-agnostic IV correction amplifies bias ($-$139\%).
113	
```

To our knowledge, this futility result (that matrix-based corrections cannot reduce covariate-induced bias under FWL decomposition) has not been explicitly stated in the measurement error literature~\\cite{carroll2006, buonaccorsi2010}.\n\n\\paragraph{Method ranking.}\nPPI++ achieves the highest RMSE reduction (47.0\\% at $K{=}3$) but requires gold labels and harms M-bias ($-$88\\%); MC-SIMEX is gold-free and $K$-robust (27--70\\%), though it harms front-door ($-$62.4\\%); rankings are stable across $K$ ($\\rho \\geq 0.80$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:correction_k_sensitivity}).\n\n\\paragraph{Correction decision procedure.}\nAlgorithm~\\ref{alg:correction} (Appendix) routes through three stages: risk assessment (exposure/IV high; M-bias low), method selection (PPI++ with gold, MC-SIMEX/DSL without), and FWL screening (skip plug-in/MLA for covariate topologies). Under topology uncertainty, ASV under candidate topologies provides worst-case sensitivity.\n\n\\paragraph{Case studies and sensitivity.}\nClinicalBERT and HumAID ($K{=}10$) confirm all patterns under linear and logistic models ($|\\Delta| < 0.02$; Appendix~\\ref{app:case_study},~\\ref{sec:correction_k_sensitivity}). End-to-end demos (Table~\\ref{tab:e2e-demo}; Appendix~\\ref{sec:real_cm_demo}) validate: PPI++ reduces exposure bias from 42.9\\% to 0.2\\%, confounding achieves 0\\% improvement (FWL), and topology-agnostic IV correction amplifies bias ($-$139\\%).\n",
      "numLines": 113,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 113
    }

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We characterized how multi-class annotation errors interact with causal DAG topology: at $K{\geq}3$, exposure and IV can reverse treatment effect signs while five topologies preserve them. ASVs yield a topology-dependent vulnerability ranking; matrix-based corrections are provably ineffective under linear regression for four covariate topologies (FWL invariance). The practical implication is direct: identify the topology first: for four topologies, no matrix-based correction helps; for exposure, PPI++ with gold labels reduces bias by 93.6\%.
5	
6	\section*{Limitations}
7	
8	We assume non-differential misclassification (Appendix~\ref{sec:nondiff_stress}). Closed-form results (Theorems~1--2) assume linear SEMs with proportional effects ($f{=}\lambda g$); proportional effects is a reasonable approximation for ordinal classification (e.g., sentiment strength) where treatment shifts the distribution along an ordered scale, and sign preservation holds at 97.3\% under non-proportional effects (Table~\ref{tab:proportional_empirical}). FWL invariance extends to logistic models for covariate topologies (Appendix~\ref{app:fwl_proof}), but for exposure, logistic specification can increase bias (Appendix~\ref{sec:fwl_nonlinear}); practical recommendations do not directly extend to nonlinear models (matching, doubly-robust). The $C(\delta)$ linear interpolation assumes misclassification patterns are structurally stable as error severity varies; LLM quality changes across prompts may not follow this path. ASV computation requires estimating $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$ from gold labels via $C^{-1}$; error propagation under small gold-label budgets ($<$500) is an open question. The framework assumes a known DAG; DAG misspecification under measurement error may compound bias. General $K{\geq}3$ envelope monotonicity remains open; worst-case step deviation reaches 0.056 for exposure but empirically decreases with $K$ ($\varepsilon \propto K^{-\alpha}$, $\alpha \in [0.6, 1.5]$; Table~\ref{tab:conjecture_verify}), and Algorithm~1 routing is robust to violations below $\varepsilon{=}0.017$.
9	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01U5QJWPpLCXyAZxQXR1Yh1D`</summary>

```text
0	\section{Proofs of Proposition~\ref{prop:signflip} and Theorem~\ref{thm:monotone}}
1	\label{app:proof}
2	
3	We provide algebraic proofs of sign preservation for the confounding and collider topologies (Proposition~\ref{prop:signflip}(a)--(b)), an explicit counterexample for exposure (Proposition~\ref{prop:signflip}(c)), numerical verification for mediation, M-bias, and front-door (Numerical Verification~1), and the monotonicity proof for confounding (Theorem~\ref{thm:monotone}).
4	Throughout, $A\in\{0,1,\dots,K{-}1\}$ with prior $p_k=P(A{=}k)$, and $C$ is a $K{\times}K$ column-stochastic confusion matrix with $C_{jk}=P(A^*{=}j\mid A{=}k)$.
5	We use category~0 as the reference and let $D_j=\mathbf{1}(A{=}j)$, $D_j^*=\mathbf{1}(A^*{=}j)$ for $j=1,\dots,K{-}1$.
6	
7	\subsection{Notation and Key Identities}
8	\label{app:notation}
9	
10	Under non-differential misclassification, the misclassified moments factor linearly through $C$.
11	Define the population vectors:
12	\begin{align}
13	  q_j &= P(A^*{=}j) = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,p_k, \label{eq:q}\\
14	  \mathrm{ct}_j &= \mathbb{E}[T\cdot D_j^*] = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,\mathbb{E}[T\cdot D_k], \label{eq:ct}\\
15	  \mathrm{cy}_j &= \mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_j^*] = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_k]. \label{eq:cy}
16	\end{align}
17	Write $q = Cp$, $\mathrm{ct} = C\,\mathbb{E}[T\cdot D]$, and $\mathrm{cy} = C\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D]$, where $D=(D_0,\dots,D_{K-1})^\top$.
18	
19	\subsection{Frisch--Waugh--Lovell Decomposition}
20	\label{app:fwl}
21	
22	For topologies where $T$ is directly observed (confounding, mediation, collider, M-bias, front-door), the estimator regresses $Y$ on $Z^*=(1,T,D_1^*,\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.
23	By the Frisch--Waugh--Lovell (FWL) theorem, the coefficient on $T$ satisfies:
24	\begin{equation}
25	  \operatorname{plim}\hat{\tau}(C)
26	  = \frac{\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,\tilde{Y}]}{\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]},
27	  \label{eq:fwl}
28	\end{equation}
29	where $\tilde{T}=T-\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]$ and $\tilde{Y}=Y-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*]$ are residuals from projecting onto $(1,D_1^*,\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.
30	
31	\begin{lemma}[Bias decomposition]
32	\label{lem:bias_decomp}
33	Suppose $Y = \tau T + f(A) + \varepsilon_Y$ with $\varepsilon_Y \perp (T, A, A^*)$.
34	Then
35	\begin{equation}
36	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) = \tau + \frac{R(C)}{D(C)},
37	  \label{eq:bias_decomp}
38	\end{equation}
39	where
40	\begin{align}
41	  R(C) &= \mathbb{E}\bigl[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*)\bigr], \label{eq:R}\\
42	  D(C) &= \mathbb{E}\bigl[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)\bigr] > 0. \label{eq:D}
43	\end{align}
44	\end{lemma}
45	
46	\begin{proof}
47	Expand $\tilde{Y}$ using $Y=\tau T + f(A) + \varepsilon_Y$:
48	\[
49	  \tilde{Y} = \tau\,\tilde{T} + \bigl(f(A) - \mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr) + \varepsilon_Y.
50	\]
51	Since $\tilde{T}$ is measurable w.r.t.\ $(T,A^*)$ and $\varepsilon_Y\perp(T,A,A^*)$:
52	\[
53	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,\tilde{Y}]
54	  = \tau\,\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]
55	  + \mathbb{E}\bigl[\tilde{T}\,\bigl(f(A)-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr)\bigr].
56	\]
57	The numerator residual is $R(C) = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}(f(A)-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*])]$, and the denominator is $D(C) = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]$.
58	
59	By the law of total covariance:
60	\begin{align*}
61	  R(C) &= \sum_{j=0}^{K-1} q_j\,\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*{=}j)\\
62	  &= \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)].
63	\end{align*}
64	For $D(C)>0$: decompose $T = g(A) + \varepsilon_T$ where $g(A)=\mathbb{E}[T\mid A]$ (for confounding) or simply note that $T$ has exogenous variation independent of $A^*$ (for mediation).
65	In both cases, $\tilde{T}=\bigl(g(A)-\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*]\bigr)+\varepsilon_T'$, where $\varepsilon_T'$ is the component of $T$ independent of $(A,A^*)$.
66	Hence $D(C)\geq\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_T')>0$.
67	\end{proof}
68	
69	\subsection{Confounding Topology ($A$ = Confounder)}
70	\label{app:confounding}
71	
72	\paragraph{Data-generating process.}
73	$A\to T$, $A\to Y$, $T\to Y$.
74	Using dummy coding with category 0 as reference:
75	\begin{align*}
76	  T &= \textstyle\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\alpha_j D_j + \varepsilon_T,\quad
77	  \varepsilon_T\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_T^2),\\
78	  Y &= \tau\, T + \textstyle\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\beta_j^{(A)} D_j + \varepsilon_Y,\\
79	  &\quad \varepsilon_Y\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),
80	\end{align*}
81	with $\varepsilon_T\perp A$ and $\varepsilon_Y\perp(T,A)$.
82	Define $g(k)=\sum_{j}\alpha_j\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$ and
83	$f(k)=\sum_{j}\beta_j^{(A)}\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$, so $g(0)=f(0)=0$.
84	
85	\paragraph{Closed-form plim.}
86	Using the FWL decomposition, the coefficient on $T$ is:
87	\begin{equation}
88	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C)
89	  = \frac{\mu_{TY} - \sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\frac{\mathrm{ct}_j\,\mathrm{cy}_j}{q_j}}
90	  {s_T - \sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\frac{\mathrm{ct}_j^2}{q_j}},
91	  \label{eq:plim_conf}
92	\end{equation}
93	where $s_T=\mathbb{E}[T^2]$, $\mu_{TY}=\mathbb{E}[TY]$, and $\mathrm{ct}_j$, $\mathrm{cy}_j$, $q_j$ are defined in~\eqref{eq:q}--\eqref{eq:cy}.
94	
95	\begin{proof}[Derivation]
96	By FWL, $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}Y]/\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]$ where $\tilde{T}=T-\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]$.
97	Since $\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*{=}j]=\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j$:
98	\begin{align*}
99	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]
100	  &= \mathbb{E}[T^2] - \textstyle\sum_j q_j\bigl(\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\bigr)^2\\
101	  &= s_T - \textstyle\sum_j \mathrm{ct}_j^2/q_j,\\
102	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,Y]
103	  &= \mathbb{E}[TY] - \textstyle\sum_j \bigl(\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\bigr)\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_j^*]\\
104	  &= \mu_{TY} - \textstyle\sum_j \mathrm{ct}_j\,\mathrm{cy}_j/q_j.
105	\end{align*}
106	\end{proof}
107	
108	\paragraph{Verification at $C=I$.}
109	When $C=I$: $q_j=p_j$, $\mathrm{ct}_j=\mathbb{E}[T D_j]=\alpha_j p_j$, $\mathrm{cy}_j=\mathbb{E}[Y D_j]=p_j(\tau\alpha_j+\beta_j^{(A)})$.
110	Direct substitution yields numerator $= \tau\sigma_T^2$ and denominator $= \sigma_T^2$, giving $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau = \tau$.
111	
112	\paragraph{Bias structure.}
113	By Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp}:
114	\begin{align}
115	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) &= \tau + B(C),\notag\\
116	  B(C) &= \frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\,f(A)\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.
117	  \label{eq:bias_conf}
118	\end{align}
119	By the law of total covariance:
120	\begin{multline}
121	  \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\mid A^*)]
122	  = \operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))\\
123	  - \operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*],\;\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr).
124	  \label{eq:lotc}
125	\end{multline}
126	
127	At $C=I$: $\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*]=g(A)$, so the between-group covariance equals $\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)$ and $B(I)=0$.
128	
129	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ (uniform, $A^*\perp A$): $\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*]$ is constant, so the between-group covariance is zero and
130	\begin{equation}
131	  B_{\mathrm{OV}} \;\equiv\; B\bigl(\tfrac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top\bigr)
132	  = \frac{\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\,f(A))}{\operatorname{Var}(T)},
133	  \label{eq:bov}
134	\end{equation}
135	which equals the classical omitted-variable bias (no control for $A$).
136	
137	\paragraph{Sign preservation condition.}
138	Since $D(C)>0$, sign$(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau) = $ sign$(\tau\cdot D(C) + R(C))$.
139	For sign preservation, it suffices that $R(C)$ does not overwhelm $\tau\cdot D(C)$.
140	
141	\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- proportional case]
142	\label{prop:conf_prop}
143	If $f(A) = \lambda\,g(A)$ for some constant $\lambda$ (i.e., the confounder effects on $T$ and $Y$ are proportional), then
144	\begin{equation}
145	  B(C) = \bigl(1-\xi(C)\bigr)\,B_{\mathrm{OV}},
146	  \label{eq:convex}
147	\end{equation}
148	where
149	\[
150	  \xi(C) = \frac{V_B\;\sigma_T^2}
151	  {V\bigl(V-V_B+\sigma_T^2\bigr)}
152	  \;\in\;[0,1],
153	\]
154	where $V_B=\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])$ and $V=\operatorname{Var}(g(A))$.
155	Consequently, $B(C)$ lies between $0$ and $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$, and $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ whenever $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau+B_{\mathrm{OV}})$.
156	\end{proposition}
157	
158	\begin{proof}
159	When $f=\lambda g$: the between-group covariance in~\eqref{eq:lotc} becomes $\lambda\,\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])$, and $\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)=\lambda\,\operatorname{Var}(g)$.
160	So $R(C)=\lambda\bigl(\operatorname{Var}(g)-\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])\bigr)=\lambda\,V_W$, where $V_W=\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B$ with $V_B=\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])$.
161	Similarly, $D(C)=V_W+\sigma_T^2$.
162	Hence
163	\[
164	  B(C) = \frac{\lambda\,V_W}{V_W+\sigma_T^2},\qquad
165	  B_{\mathrm{OV}} = \frac{\lambda\,V}{V+\sigma_T^2},
166	\]
167	where $V=\operatorname{Var}(g)=V_B+V_W$.
168	The ratio
169	\[
170	  \frac{B(C)}{B_{\mathrm{OV}}} = \frac{V_W(V+\sigma_T^2)}{V(V_W+\sigma_T^2)} = 1 - \xi(C)
171	\]
172	is a decreasing function of $V_B\in[0,V]$.
173	At $V_B=0$ (no classification, $C$ uniform): $\xi=0$, $B=B_{\mathrm{OV}}$.
174	At $V_B=V$ (perfect classification, $C=I$): $\xi=1$, $B=0$.
175	Since $0\leq V_B\leq V$, we have $\xi\in[0,1]$ and $B(C)\in[0,B_{\mathrm{OV}}]$ (with the sign of $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$).
176	\end{proof}
177	
178	\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- general case]
179	\label{prop:conf_gen}
180	For general $g$ and $f$ (not necessarily proportional), define the worst-case bias bound
181	\begin{equation}
182	  \bar{B} \;=\; \frac{\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\,\operatorname{Var}(f(A))}}{\sigma_T^2}.
183	  \label{eq:bbar}
184	\end{equation}
185	If $|\tau| > \bar{B}$, then $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ for all column-stochastic $C$.
186	\end{proposition}
187	
188	\begin{proof}
189	We need $\operatorname{sign}(\tau\cdot D(C)+R(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$, which holds if $|R(C)|<|\tau|\cdot D(C)$.
190	Since $D(C)\geq\sigma_T^2$ (shown in Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp}), it suffices to show $|R(C)|<|\tau|\,\sigma_T^2$.
191	
192	\emph{Step 1: Bound $|R(C)|$.}
193	$R(C)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A)\mid A^*)]$.
194	By the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality applied to each within-group covariance:
195	\begin{multline*}
196	  |\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\mid A^*{=}j)|\\
197	  \leq \sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g\mid A^*{=}j)\,\operatorname{Var}(f\mid A^*{=}j)}.
198	\end{multline*}
199	Taking expectations and applying Cauchy--Schwarz again:
